\bm{\Omega}=\mathbb{B}_{3}(R)\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a ball off radius
R
which supports a perfect fluid/gas of constant density
\rho(r)=\rho
and pressure
p(r)
and total mass
M
. Then

(10.63)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(r)=\int_{0}^{r}4\pi\rho\bar{r}^{2}d\bar{r}=\frac{4}{3}\pi r^{3}\rho

and
M=\mathcal{M}(R)=\tfrac{4}{3}\pi R^{3}\rho
. The TOVE is then integrable so that:

(1)

The pressure within the star is

(10.64)

\displaystyle p(r)=\frac{3M}{4\pi R^{3}}\frac{[1-(2\mathscr{G}M/R)]^{1/2}-[1-2\mathscr{G}Mr^{2}/R^{3}]^{1/2}}{[1-(2\mathscr{G}Mr^{2}/R^{3})]^{1/2}-3[1-2\mathscr{G}M/R]^{1/2}}

or equivalently

(10.65)

\displaystyle p(x)=\rho\frac{\sqrt{1-x^{2}}-\sqrt{1-x_{o}^{2}}}{3\sqrt{1-x_{o}^{2}}-\sqrt{1-x^{2}}}

where
x=r\sqrt{\tfrac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}\rho}
and
x_{o}=R\sqrt{\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}\rho}=\sqrt{2\mathscr{G}M/R^{2}}=r_{0}/R
, where
r_{0}
is the square root of the event horizon radius
R_{o}=\sqrt{2\mathscr{G}M}
.

(2)

The central pressure for
r=0
or
x=0
is

(10.66)

\displaystyle p_{c}=p(0)=\frac{3M}{4\pi R^{3}}\left(\frac{1-(2\mathscr{G}M/R))^{1/2}-1}{1-3[1-2\mathscr{G}M/R)^{1/2}}\right)

or equivalently

(10.67)

\displaystyle p_{c}=p(0)=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-r_{0}/R}}{3\sqrt{1-r_{0}/R-1}}

(3)

The central pressure is infinite if
\mathscr{G}M/R=4/9
so that there is a sharp upper bound

(10.68)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathscr{G}M}{R}<\frac{4}{9}

Proof.

Rewrite the TOVE with
\rho(r)=\rho
so that

\displaystyle\frac{dp(r)}{dr}=-\frac{\mathscr{G}\mathcal{M}(r)\rho(r)}{r^{2}}\left(1+\frac{p(r)}{\rho(r)}\right)\left(1+\frac{4\pi r^{3}p(r)}{\mathcal{M}(r)}\right)\left(1-\frac{2\mathscr{G}\mathcal{M}(r)}{r}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathscr{G}}{r^{2}}[\frac{4}{3}\pi r^{3}\rho][p(r)+\rho]\left(1+\frac{4\pi r^{3}p(r)}{\frac{4}{3}\pi r^{3}\rho}\right)\left(1-\frac{2\mathscr{G}}{r}\frac{4}{3}\pi r^{3}\rho\right)^{-1}

(10.69)

\displaystyle=4\pi\mathscr{G}r[\rho+p(r)][\frac{\rho}{3}+p(r)]\left[1-\frac{8}{3}\mathscr{G}r^{2}\rho\right]^{-1}

This expression is then integrated
\emph{inwards}
from the surface at
r=R
where
p(R)=0
so that

(10.70)

\displaystyle-\int_{p(R)=0}^{p(r)}d\bar{p(r)}(p(r)+\rho)^{-1}(\frac{1}{3}\rho+\bar{p(r)})^{-1}=\int_{p(r)}^{0}d\bar{p(r)}(p(r)+\rho)^{-1}(\frac{1}{3}\rho+\bar{p(r)})^{-1}

(10.71)

\displaystyle=\int_{R}^{r}4\pi\mathscr{G}\bar{r}\left[1-\frac{8}{3}\mathscr{G}\bar{r}^{2}\rho\right]d\bar{r}

Performing both integrals gives

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2\rho}\log\frac{\rho+p(r)}{3p(r)+\rho}=-\frac{3}{4\rho}\log\left|\frac{\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}r^{2}-1}{\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}R^{2}-1}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4\rho}\log\left|\frac{\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}R^{2}-1}{\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}r^{2}-1}\right|=\frac{3}{4\rho}\log\left|\frac{-[\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}R^{2}-1]}{-[\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}r^{2}-1]}\right|

(10.72)

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4\rho}\log\left|\frac{[1-\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}R^{2}]}{[1-\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}r^{2}]}\right|

Hence

(10.73)

\displaystyle\log\left|\frac{\rho+p(r)}{3p(r)+\rho}\right|=\frac{1}{2}\log\left|\frac{[1-\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}R^{2}]}{[1-\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}r^{2}]}\right|=\log\left|\frac{[1-\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}R^{2}]}{[1-\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}r^{2}]}\right|^{1/2}

so that

(10.74)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\rho+p(r)}{3p(r)+\rho}\right|=\left|\frac{[1-\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}R^{2}]}{[1-\frac{8}{3}\pi\mathscr{G}r^{2}]}\right|^{1/2}

Using
\rho=3M/4\pi R^{3}
for
r<R
and solving for
p(r)
then gives formula (-) for the pressure. Setting
r=0
or
x=0
then gives the formulas (-) or (-) for the central pressure. The central pressure becomes infinite if

(10.75)

\displaystyle 3\sqrt{1-r_{o}/R}-1=0

which is
\mathscr{G}M/R=4/9
. This then establishes the Buchdal bound (10.68).
∎

Remark 10.6
.

It can be seen for any sufficiently massive star, regardless of how exotic its matter composition (Eg., ’quark stars’) and regardless of it equation of state, can reach a density such that
GM/R=4/9
at which point it would require an infinite pressure to support it against its own gravity. Gravity then totally dominates and the star cannot be physically supported. It will then collapses through its event horizon radius to a point, essentially becoming a black hole. Note also that in the Newtonian case with constant density, the central pressure can only become infinite when
R=0
whereas in the relativistic case, the infinite central pressure will occur for a finite radius for which
R=\tfrac{9}{4}\mathscr{G}M
.
